```
295	@@ -0,0 +1,287 @@
296	+"""Structured validation for graph marginalisation / posterior inference."""
297	+
298	+from __future__ import annotations
299	+
300	+from dataclasses import dataclass, field
301	+from typing import Any
302	+
303	+from core.node_data import manual_posterior_override_enabled, node_question_id
304	+from core.research_driver.bn_inference import _ancestor_closure, _build_parent_maps, _topological_order_or_raise
305	+from core.research_driver.data_model import BayesianGraph
306	+
307	+_EPSILON = 1e-9
308	+
309	+
310	+@dataclass(frozen=True)
311	+class MarginalisationValidationIssue:
312	+    code: str
313	+    message: str
314	+    subject_id: str
315	+    subject_type: str
316	+    label: str = ""
317	+    details: dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)
318	+
319	+
320	+def _node_id(node: Any) -> str:
321	+    if hasattr(node, "node_id"):
322	+        return str(getattr(node, "node_id") or "").strip()
323	+    return str(getattr(node, "id", "") or "").strip()
324	+
325	+
326	+def _node_label(node: Any) -> str:
327	+    return str(getattr(node, "label", "") or "").strip()
328	+
329	+
330	+def _node_type(node: Any) -> str:
331	+    raw = str(getattr(node, "type", "") or "").strip().lower()
332	+    if raw == "option":
333	+        return "target"
334	+    return raw
335	+
336	+
337	+def _node_data(node: Any) -> dict[str, Any]:
338	+    data = getattr(node, "data", None)
339	+    return dict(data) if isinstance(data, dict) else {}
340	+
341	+
342	+def _node_baseline_p(node: Any) -> float | None:
343	+    raw = getattr(node, "baseline_p", None)
344	+    if raw is None:
345	+        return None
346	+    try:
347	+        return float(raw)
348	+    except (TypeError, ValueError):
349	+        return None
350	+
351	+
352	+def _edge_id(edge: Any) -> str:
353	+    return str(getattr(edge, "edge_id", None) or getattr(edge, "id", "") or "").strip()
354	+
355	+
356	+def _edge_type(edge: Any) -> str:
357	+    raw = getattr(edge, "type", None)
358	+    if raw is None:
359	+        raw = getattr(edge, "toi", None)
360	+    return str(raw or "").strip().upper()
361	+
362	+
363	+def _edge_from(edge: Any) -> str:
364	+    return str(getattr(edge, "from_node", None) or getattr(edge, "from_id", "") or "").strip()
365	+
366	+
367	+def _edge_to(edge: Any) -> str:
368	+    return str(getattr(edge, "to_node", None) or getattr(edge, "to_id", "") or "").strip()
369	+
370	+
371	+def _edge_weight(edge: Any) -> float | None:
372	+    raw = getattr(edge, "weight", None)
373	+    if raw is None:
374	+        return None
375	+    try:
376	+        return float(raw)
377	+    except (TypeError, ValueError):
378	+        return None
379	+
380	+
381	+def _question_nodes_for_id(nodes: list[Any], *, question_id: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
382	+    qid = str(question_id or "").strip()
383	+    out: dict[str, Any] = {}
384	+    for node in nodes:
385	+        node_id = _node_id(node)
386	+        if not node_id:
387	+            continue
388	+        if node_question_id(_node_data(node)) != qid:
389	+            continue
390	+        out[node_id] = node
391	+    return out
392	+
393	+
394	+def validate_graph_for_marginalisation(
395	+    *,
396	+    question_id: str,
397	+    nodes: list[Any],
398	+    edges: list[Any],
399	+) -> list[MarginalisationValidationIssue]:
400	+    """Return structured validation issues; empty list means marginalisation may run."""
401	+    qid = str(question_id or "").strip()
402	+    issues: list[MarginalisationValidationIssue] = []
403	+    if not qid:
404	+        issues.append(
405	+            MarginalisationValidationIssue(
406	+                code="missing_question_id",
407	+                message="question_id is required for marginalisation validation.",
408	+                subject_id="",
409	+                subject_type="question",
410	+            )
411	+        )
412	+        return issues
413	+
414	+    question_nodes = _question_nodes_for_id(nodes, question_id=qid)
415	+    target_nodes = [node for node in question_nodes.values() if _node_type(node) == "target"]
416	+    if not target_nodes:
417	+        issues.append(
418	+            MarginalisationValidationIssue(
419	+                code="no_targets",
420	+                message="Question has no target nodes; marginalisation cannot run.",
421	+                subject_id=qid,
422	+                subject_type="question",
423	+            )
424	+        )
425	+        return issues
426	+
427	+    target_ids = sorted(_node_id(node) for node in target_nodes if _node_id(node))
428	+
429	+    for edge in edges:
430	+        if _edge_type(edge) != "CONDITIONAL":
431	+            continue
432	+        edge_id = _edge_id(edge) or f"{_edge_from(edge)}->{_edge_to(edge)}"
433	+        from_id = _edge_from(edge)
434	+        to_id = _edge_to(edge)
435	+        if from_id not in question_nodes and to_id not in question_nodes:
436	+            continue
437	+        if from_id not in question_nodes or to_id not in question_nodes:
438	+            missing = to_id if to_id not in question_nodes else from_id
439	+            issues.append(
440	+                MarginalisationValidationIssue(
441	+                    code="edge_missing_endpoint",
442	+                    message="Conditional edge references a node that is not in the question graph.",
443	+                    subject_id=edge_id,
444	+                    subject_type="edge",
445	+                    details={"from_node": from_id, "to_node": to_id, "missing_node_id": missing},
446	+                )
447	+            )
448	+            continue
449	+        weight = _edge_weight(edge)
450	+        if weight is None or weight <= 0.0:
451	+            issues.append(
452	+                MarginalisationValidationIssue(
453	+                    code="non_positive_edge_weight",
454	+                    message="Conditional edge weight must be a positive number.",
455	+                    subject_id=edge_id,
456	+                    subject_type="edge",
457	+                    details={"from_node": from_id, "to_node": to_id, "weight": weight},
458	+                )
459	+            )
460	+
461	+    conditional_edges: list[tuple[str, str, float]] = []
462	+    seen_weights: dict[tuple[str, str], float] = {}
463	+    for edge in edges:
464	+        if _edge_type(edge) != "CONDITIONAL":
465	+            continue
466	+        from_id = _edge_from(edge)
467	+        to_id = _edge_to(edge)
468	+        if from_id not in question_nodes or to_id not in question_nodes:
469	+            continue
470	+        weight = _edge_weight(edge)
471	+        if weight is None or weight <= 0.0:
472	+            continue
473	+        edge_id = _edge_id(edge) or f"{from_id}->{to_id}"
474	+        key = (from_id, to_id)
475	+        if key in seen_weights and abs(seen_weights[key] - weight) > _EPSILON:
476	+            issues.append(
477	+                MarginalisationValidationIssue(
478	+                    code="duplicate_conflicting_conditional_edge",
479	+                    message="Duplicate conditional edges with conflicting weights are not allowed.",
480	+                    subject_id=edge_id,
481	+                    subject_type="edge",
482	+                    details={
483	+                        "from_node": from_id,
484	+                        "to_node": to_id,
485	+                        "existing_weight": seen_weights[key],
486	+                        "conflicting_weight": weight,
487	+                    },
488	+                )
489	+            )
490	+            continue
491	+        seen_weights[key] = weight
492	+        conditional_edges.append((from_id, to_id, weight))
493	+
494	+    parents_by_child, _children_by_parent = _build_parent_maps(
495	+        conditional_edges,
496	+        active_nodes=set(question_nodes),
497	+    )
498	+    active_nodes = _ancestor_closure(targets=target_ids, parents_by_child=parents_by_child)
499	+    active_edges = [(s, d, w) for s, d, w in conditional_edges if s in active_nodes and d in active_nodes]
500	+
501	+    try:
502	+        _topological_order_or_raise(active_nodes=active_nodes, edges=active_edges)
503	+    except ValueError as exc:
504	+        issues.append(
505	+            MarginalisationValidationIssue(
506	+                code="conditional_cycle",
507	+                message=str(exc),
508	+                subject_id=qid,
509	+                subject_type="question",
510	+            )
511	+        )
512	+
513	+    for node_id in sorted(active_nodes):
514	+        node = question_nodes[node_id]
515	+        node_type = _node_type(node)
516	+        if node_type == "target":
517	+            parents = parents_by_child.get(node_id, [])
518	+            if not parents and not manual_posterior_override_enabled(_node_data(node)):
519	+                issues.append(
520	+                    MarginalisationValidationIssue(
521	+                        code="isolated_target_without_override",
522	+                        message=("Target is not connected to the active Bayesian network and has no manual posterior override."),
523	+                        subject_id=node_id,
524	+                        subject_type="node",
525	+                        label=_node_label(node),
526	+                    )
527	+                )
528	+            continue
529	+
530	+        parents = parents_by_child.get(node_id, [])
531	+        has_baseline = _node_baseline_p(node) is not None
532	+        has_override = manual_posterior_override_enabled(_node_data(node))
533	+        if not parents and not has_baseline and not has_override:
534	+            issues.append(
535	+                MarginalisationValidationIssue(
536	+                    code="missing_node_prior",
537	+                    message=("Active node has no parent inputs, baseline prior, or manual posterior override."),
538	+                    subject_id=node_id,
539	+                    subject_type="node",
540	+                    label=_node_label(node),
541	+                    details={"node_type": node_type},
542	+                )
543	+            )
544	+
545	+    if len(target_ids) > 1:
546	+        missing_index = [
547	+            _node_id(node)
548	+            for node in target_nodes
549	+            if _node_id(node)
550	+            and not isinstance(_node_data(node).get("detail", {}).get("option_index"), int)
551	+            and not isinstance(_node_data(node).get("option_index"), int)
552	+        ]
553	+        if missing_index:
554	+            issues.append(
555	+                MarginalisationValidationIssue(
556	+                    code="target_exclusivity_metadata",
557	+                    message="Multi-target questions need option_index metadata for mutual exclusivity normalization.",
558	+                    subject_id=qid,
559	+                    subject_type="question",
560	+                    details={"target_ids": missing_index},
561	+                )
562	+            )
563	+
564	+    return issues
565	+
566	+
567	+def validate_graph_object_for_marginalisation(
568	+    graph: BayesianGraph,
569	+    *,
570	+    question_id: str | None = None,
571	+) -> list[MarginalisationValidationIssue]:
572	+    qid = str(question_id or getattr(graph, "question_id", "") or "").strip()
573	+    nodes = list((getattr(graph, "nodes", {}) or {}).values())
574	+    edges = list((getattr(graph, "edges", {}) or {}).values())
575	+    return validate_graph_for_marginalisation(question_id=qid, nodes=nodes, edges=edges)
576	+
577	+
578	+__all__ = [
579	+    "MarginalisationValidationIssue",
580	+    "validate_graph_for_marginalisation",
581	+    "validate_graph_object_for_marginalisation",
582	+]

708	+from __future__ import annotations

719	+
720	+
721	+def _node_record(
722	+    *,
723	+    node_id: str,
724	+    node_type: str,
725	+    question_id: str,
726	+    baseline_p: float | None = 0.5,
727	+    label: str = "",
728	+    data_extra: dict | None = None,
729	+) -> NodeRecord:
730	+    data: dict = {"question_id": question_id}
731	+    if node_type == "target":
732	+        data["detail"] = {"option_index": 0}
733	+    if data_extra:
734	+        data.update(data_extra)
735	+    return NodeRecord(
736	+        node_id=node_id,
737	+        type=node_type,
738	+        label=label or node_id,
739	+        toi=None,
740	+        baseline_p=baseline_p,
741	+        data=data,
742	+    )
743	+
744	+
745	+def _edge_record(*, edge_id: str, from_node: str, to_node: str, weight: float = 2.0) -> EdgeRecord:
746	+    return EdgeRecord(
747	+        edge_id=edge_id,
748	+        from_node=from_node,
749	+        to_node=to_node,
750	+        toi="CONDITIONAL",
751	+        weight=weight,
752	+        type="CONDITIONAL",
753	+        data={"question_id": "q_val"},
754	+    )
755	+

758	+    nodes = [

767	+        ),
768	+    ]
769	+    issues = validate_graph_for_marginalisation(question_id="q_val", nodes=nodes, edges=[])
770	+    codes = {issue.code for issue in issues}
771	+    assert "isolated_target_without_override" in codes
772	+
```

```
336	    *,

340	) -> dict[str, float]:
341	    forced_one = [target_id for target_id in target_ids if int(evidence_values.get(target_id, -1)) == 1]
342	    if len(forced_one) > 1:
343	        raise ValueError("Target evidence is inconsistent with mutual exclusivity (multiple targets forced to 1).")
344	    if len(forced_one) == 1:
345	        chosen = forced_one[0]
346	        return {target_id: 1.0 if target_id == chosen else 0.0 for target_id in target_ids}
347	
348	    forced_zero = {target_id for target_id in target_ids if int(evidence_values.get(target_id, -1)) == 0}
349	    allowed = [target_id for target_id in target_ids if target_id not in forced_zero]
350	    if not allowed:
351	        raise ValueError("Target evidence is inconsistent with mutual exclusivity (all targets forced to 0).")
352	
353	    total = 0.0
354	    positive_raw: dict[str, float] = {}
355	    for target_id in allowed:
356	        value = max(0.0, float(raw_target_posteriors.get(target_id, 0.0)))
357	        positive_raw[target_id] = value
358	        total += value
359	
360	    out: dict[str, float] = {target_id: 0.0 for target_id in target_ids}

362	        uniform = 1.0 / float(len(allowed))
363	        for target_id in allowed:
364	            out[target_id] = uniform
365	        return out
366	
367	    for target_id in allowed:
368	        out[target_id] = float(positive_raw[target_id] / total)
```

```
# Agent Instructions

These instructions apply to coding agents (Cursor, Claude Code, Codex) in this
repository. Follow them unless the user gives a newer explicit instruction in
the conversation.

## Canonical instruction source

- `AGENTS.md` is the canonical shared instruction file.
- Codex, Claude Code, and Cursor read `AGENTS.md` directly.
- Do not duplicate these rules into `.cursor/rules/`, `CLAUDE.md`, or other
  agent instruction files. Thin `.cursor/rules/*.mdc` **routing pointers** to
  canonical docs (for example `codequality.md`) are allowed.
- Keep this file concise. Long protocols, skills, schemas, and run records live
  under `agent_rules/`.

## Branch workflow (detect first)

Run `git branch --show-current` before any git or PR action.

| Branch | Default workflow |
| --- | --- |
| `dev_sprint` | Edit → minimal verify → **commit directly** to `dev_sprint`. No PR unless the user asks. |
| `dev` | Edit on a **feature branch** off `dev` → minimal verify → push → open a **draft** PR targeting `dev`. **Do not commit directly to `dev`** unless the user explicitly asks. |
| other | Stop and confirm the intended branch with the user. |

Porting selected capabilities from `dev_sprint` into `dev` is a separate flow
(capability diff / port skills), not ordinary work on `dev`.

### PRs targeting `dev`

- **Always create PRs as draft** (`gh pr create --draft` or equivalent).
- **All required GitHub checks must pass** before the PR is eligible to leave
  draft. Fix failures, push, and re-check until green.
- Mark ready for review only after required GitHub checks are green (see
  `agent_rules/pr_workflow.md`).
- Never merge a PR with failing or pending required checks.
- Always tag `@kungfusaini` on every PR.

`dev_sprint` rarely uses PRs. Do not open PRs to `dev_sprint` unless the user
asks.

## Task tiers (context discipline)

Choose a tier from the user prompt. Default to **S** when unclear.

| Tier | When | Required |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **S** | Single area, roughly ≤5 files, user named the change | Branch rule, `git status --short`, read named paths, **first code edit within the first few tool rounds**, minimal verify, commit or draft PR per branch |
| **M** | Multi-file feature, one session | Tier S + short plan (≤5 bullets) in chat; targeted tests; no new run record unless multi-session |
| **L** | Cross-FS, gated, multi-session, Codex batches | Full `agent_rules/run_protocol.md`, run record, skills as needed, full `pr_workflow.md` before PR push |

### Delivery rules (all tiers)

- **Done** means working code in the repo (commit on `dev_sprint` or draft PR on
  `dev`), with verification run for the touched area — not plans, run-record
  essays, or checklists recited in chat.
- Do **not** create or update `agent_rules/runs/` for tier **S** unless the user
  names a run id.
- Do **not** read `docs/plans/` before the first edit unless the user asked for
  design review or tier **L** planning.
- Prefer ≤3 file reads before the first edit unless locating a symbol.
- Keep status messages short between tool batches; put long analysis in files
  only when the user asked for a report.

### Code bloat (mandatory)

- **Minimal diff**: solve the asked problem with the smallest correct change.
- **No drive-by refactors**, renames, formatting sweeps, or unrelated cleanup.
- **No new abstractions** (helpers, base classes, indirection) unless the user
  asked or the change cannot be done cleanly inline.
- **Reuse existing** functions, patterns, and types; extend before adding parallel
  implementations.
- **Do not expand scope** to “while we’re here” items without explicit user
  approval.
- **One logical change** per commit or PR.

## Pointers (tier-gated)

| Topic | Read when |
```

> AGENT

I have what I need. Here's my review of PR #432.

## Review: PR #432 — Graph marginalisation validation and manual override inference

Overall the change is well-structured: clear precedence, focused tests, and the override semantics match the docstring. A handful of issues worth addressing before this leaves draft:

### Blockers / clear bugs

**1. `build_manual_posterior_override` clamp is silently undone by the round.** (`core/node_data.py:54-60`)
```python
clamped = min(1.0 - 1e-9, max(1e-9, float(posterior_p)))
return {
    ...
    "posterior_p": round(clamped, 6),
```
`round(1.0 - 1e-9, 6)` is `1.0`, and `round(1e-9, 6)` is `0.0`. The explicit clamp does nothing. Two consequences:
- `_write_posteriors_to_graph` writes `round(float(override_p), 6)` straight to `node.posterior_p` without `_clip_probability`, so an override of `1.0`/`0.0` lands as-is on the graph and any downstream logit/log-odds consumer will blow up.
- The propagation path is safe because `_propagate_posteriors` applies `_clip_probability`, but you have two inconsistent code paths. Either clip on write-back, or round-then-clamp (not clamp-then-round), or use `round(min(..., max(..., p)), 6)` and accept the asymmetry — but pick one.

**2. `without_manual_posterior_override` doesn't remove anything.** (`core/node_data.py:79-85`) It sets `enabled = False` while leaving the rest of the payload. The name implies removal — either rename to `disable_manual_posterior_override` or actually pop the key. The current name will mislead a future caller who expects `"manual_posterior_override" not in data` after calling it.

### Code-quality concerns

**3. Reaching into private helpers across modules.** (`core/research_driver/graph_marginalisation_validation.py:4`)
```python
from core.research_driver.bn_inference import _ancestor_closure, _build_parent_maps, _topological_order_or_raise
```
Importing leading-underscore symbols from another module is a smell — the privacy marker means they aren't part of the contract. Either promote them to public names (drop the underscore, add to `__all__`) or duplicate the small bits of logic into validation. Since you're adding a new public surface that depends on them, I'd promote.

**4. PR title prefix is wrong.** `pr_workflow.md:50-54` only allows `FS<N>:`, `Cross-FS:`, or `#<issue>:`. `UI:` isn't a permitted prefix — this looks like FS-something or Cross-FS work.

### Smaller nits

- `core/research_driver/bn_inference.py:151` — the new import line is very long; wrap it.
- `_normalize_exclusive_targets` silently drops `manual_fixed_targets` entries that are also `forced_zero`. Probably fine (evidence trumps override) but worth a comment or, better, raise — currently a user override pointing at an evidence-zero target is just discarded with no signal.
- `target_exclusivity_metadata` check uses `isinstance(..., int)`. If `option_index` ever round-trips as a JSON string from storage you'll get false positives. Worth verifying the loader normalizes this, or accepting `int | str-that-parses-as-int`.
- Inconsistent data extraction style in `bn_inference.py`: some sites use `getattr(node, "data", {}) or {}`, others `getattr(node, "data", {}).get("iw_trigger") or {}` (no outer `or {}`). Pick one.

### Things I liked
- Precedence is documented at the top of `bn_inference.py` and matches the code.
- Tests cover the meaningful cases: override > IW, disable restores computed, target overrides participate in exclusivity normalization (with the renormalization math actually verified at `0.8/0.2`).
- `MarginalisationValidationIssue` as a structured dataclass with `code` is a much better surface for the eventual UI than free-text errors.

Want me to push fixes for (1) and (2) as suggestions, or just leave the review as comments on the PR?

```
1	 ## Code quality patterns
2	
3	  ### Anti-patterns — reject on sight
4	
5	  - **Inline system prompts** — prompts must live in `prompts/` and load via `default_prompt_store().load(...)`.
6	  Never `_SYSTEM_PROMPT = "..."`.
7	  - **JSON files as storage** — all persistent state goes through the database via `packages/storage/`. JSON file
8	  storage is retired.
9	  - **Defensive string wrapping on comparisons** — `str(x or "").strip().upper() == "CONDITIONAL"` adds nothing over
10	   `x == "CONDITIONAL"`. Reject it.
11	  - **Global mutation + lazy imports** — `global _x` inside a function body with an inline import is a scratch
12	  pattern. Revert it. If a circular import forces deferred loading, use a function-scoped `from X import Y`
13	  (Python caches imports after first call). Never use module-level `Any | None` sentinels with `global`
14	  mutation and tuple-of-`Any` return values — it destroys type information for no benefit.
15	  - **Dropped model resolution fallback** — use `model or resolve_role_model("tool")`, not `str(model or
16	  "").strip()`.
17	  - **Module-level model resolution constants** — `MY_MODEL = resolve_task_model("FS3.task")` at module scope
18	  resolves at import time and exports an internal detail as a public constant. Call `resolve_task_model()`
19	  at the point of use. Tests should call it directly, not import a "compatibility alias".
20	  - **ORM Row class defined outside `packages/storage/database.py`** — every SQLAlchemy `Base` subclass must
21	  live in `packages/storage/database.py`. Defining a Row class in a domain module (e.g. `fitted_params_store.py`)
22	  makes the table invisible to the shared Base until that module is imported, silently allows tables to
23	  diverge from the canonical schema inventory, and hides the class from the `__all__` export in `database.py`.
24	  - **No-op list comprehensions and indiscriminate type coercion** — `[item for item in list(x or [])]` is
25	  just `list(x or [])`. And wrapping every DB field in `str(row.field or "")` regardless of nullability
26	  obscures which fields are actually nullable. Coerce only where the source type genuinely differs from the
27	  target type; don't apply `str()` / `int()` / `float()` uniformly to fields that are already the correct
28	  type.
29	  - **Dead code left behind** — if a function appears to have no callers, flag it. Grep for callers first; if
30	  there are none, confirm with the user before removing. Don't silently leave dead code with "compatibility
31	  alias" or "legacy" comments, but don't silently delete it either.
32	  - **`os.environ` deep in domain code** — environment variables should be read at the edges (config loading,
33	  CLI entry points, runtime initialization) and threaded through as typed config. Checking
34	  `os.environ.get("LITELLM_PROXY_BASE_URL")` inside a function that builds LLM messages means behavior
35	  changes silently based on env state, is untestable without monkeypatching `os.environ`, and buries
36	  configuration decisions where no one will find them.
37	  - **One file, one concern** — a module that mixes type definitions, algorithm logic, AND large blocks
38	  of declarative configuration data is doing too much. Split by rate-of-change: data that domain experts
39	  tweak (metric expectations, profiles, registries, thresholds) belongs in its own file, separate from
40	  the code that consumes it. Example: `fs10_validation/tuning.py` originally held `TuneSpec` /
41	  `run_tune()` plus a ~200-line `DEFAULT_PROFILES` table of per-feature-set baseline metrics. The
42	  profiles were moved to `fs10_validation/tuning_profiles.py` because tweaking expected metric values
43	  has nothing to do with the tuning algorithm. Same rule for surface registries, prompt catalogues,
44	  gate sequence definitions — if it's data that lives alongside code, ask whether the data has its own
45	  reason to change.
46	  - **Per-file `_storage_config(tmp_path)` helpers instead of a pytest fixture** — when
47	  every test file defines its own `def _storage_config(tmp_path: Path) -> StorageRuntimeConfig:`
48	  helper that just news up a `StorageRuntimeConfig` with the same boilerplate, the duplication
49	  is wrong twice over: (1) it's literal copy-paste across many files, and (2) it bypasses
50	  pytest's whole reason to exist — fixtures. Hoist to `tests/conftest.py` (or
51	  `tests/support/storage.py`) as `@pytest.fixture def storage_config(tmp_path: Path)
52	  -> StorageRuntimeConfig`. Tests then declare the parameter and pytest injects it. Same
53	  rule for any private test helper that takes only fixtures and returns a fixture-shaped
54	  value — that's a fixture, not a helper.
55	  - **One module straddling many feature-set boundaries** — if a single file's import
56	  block reaches into 5+ feature-set namespaces (FS2 + FS10 + FS11 + core graph +
57	  artifacts in one go), the module is doing too much. The "kitchen sink" split that
58	  groups every authority touched by a feature-set into one mega-file makes it impossible
59	  to deploy or test sub-areas in isolation. Split per *authority*, not per feature-set:
60	  `fs2_baseline_authority.py`, `fs2_current_resolution_authority.py`,
61	  `fs2_process_authority.py`, etc. Each imports only what it needs. Same rule as the
62	  `tuning.py` / `tuning_profiles.py` split — one module, one cohesion unit.
63	  - **ORM Row classes leaking into domain code** — when domain modules import
64	  `BaselinePriceMetricSeriesRow` / `NodeRow` / `EdgeRow` directly from
65	  `packages/storage/database.py` and reach for `row.target_node_id`,
66	  `row.provider_attempt_id`, etc., the SQLAlchemy ORM types are leaking past the
67	  persistence boundary. Domain code should consume **stored dataclasses** (e.g.
68	  `StoredBaselinePriceMetricSample`) returned by a **repository** that owns the Row
69	  type. Pattern: `packages/storage/baseline.py` exposes
70	  `BaselinePriceMetricRepository.list_for_question(question_id) -> list[StoredBaselinePriceMetricSample]`
71	  and the Row class never appears in any caller's import block. Fixes the import-wall
72	  symptom *and* makes the domain code testable without a SQLAlchemy session.
73	  - **Blanket `except Exception` with `# noqa: BLE001` and a "must not fail" comment** — patterns
74	  like `except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 - runtime sync must not fail the question run`
75	  followed by `errors.append(...)` swallow programmer bugs (`KeyError`, `TypeError`,
76	  `AttributeError`, schema drift) the same way they swallow expected errors (`ValueError`,
77	  `SQLAlchemyError`). The "don't fail" decision belongs at the *caller's* edge (e.g. the worker
78	  pipeline), not buried inside the persistence layer. Inside the persistence helper, catch only
79	  the exceptions you actually expect (`ValueError`, `SQLAlchemyError`) and let programmer errors
80	  propagate so tests catch them. The `# noqa` is a tell that the linter already flagged this.
81	  - **Magic schema-version literals inside dict-literal payloads** — bad: `outcome_value={
82	  "schema_version": "fs2_runtime_historical_summary_ref_v1", ...}` baked into a function body.
83	  Lift the version string to a module constant (`_FS2_HISTORICAL_SUMMARY_SCHEMA = "fs2_runtime_..._v1"`)
84	  or, better, define a typed payload dataclass / TypedDict that owns the schema version and
85	  stamps it on `to_dict()`. Same rule for any string of the shape `<thing>_v<digit>` appearing
86	  inline in an assignment.
87	  - **`Mapping[str, Any]` payloads with `str(payload.get("k") or "").strip()` everywhere** — when
88	  a function reads many keys off a free-form mapping and coerces each one defensively, the
89	  underlying problem is that the mapping should have been a TypedDict. The coercion soup is
90	  symptomatic. Promote the payload to a TypedDict (or dataclass) at the boundary; the coercion
91	  disappears because the keys are now typed at their source.
92	  - **Defensive `.get(key, magic_default)` against a dict whose keys are guaranteed by an upstream
93	  invariant** — when a validator higher up the call stack guarantees all required keys are present
94	  (e.g. `_validate_odds_multiplier_mapping` raises if any `ALLOWED_RELATIONSHIP_JUDGMENTS` label is
95	  missing), reaching for the value with `.get(key, fallback)` instead of `[key]` does two bad
96	  things at once: (a) the magic fallback is dead code by construction, and (b) when the upstream
97	  invariant *does* regress, the `.get` silently returns the magic value instead of crashing —
98	  exactly when you most want to know. Example: `relationship_judgment_odds_multiplier` returns
99	  `_authoritative_odds_multiplier_mapping().get(judgment, 2.0)` even though `judgment` always
100	  comes from `ALLOWED_RELATIONSHIP_JUDGMENTS` and every label is required to be in the mapping.
101	  The `2.0` is also unexplained — it happens to coincide with the TOML's "med (for)" default,
102	  which means an unrelated TOML edit silently desyncs the fallback. Right shape:
103	  `mapping[judgment]` — let it raise; that's how you find out the validator broke. Pair with the
104	  rule against magic numerical literals: any unexplained `1.0` / `2.0` / `0.5` / `100` in arithmetic
105	  or as a `.get` default needs either a named module constant with rationale, or — better — to be
106	  removed because the call path makes it unreachable.
107	  - **Typed-Pydantic → `dict.get("k")` → typed-TypedDict bridges** — sharper variant of the
108	  coercion-soup pattern: the *source* is a Pydantic model with typed fields and the *destination*
109	  is a TypedDict, but the assembling code does `payload = dict(result); payload.get("causal_validity")`
110	  in between, deliberately throwing both ends' types away. Example from
111	  `core/research_driver/conditional_edge_quality.py:90-105` where
112	  `_ConditionalEdgeQualityOutput(BaseModel)` (with `causal_validity: int | None = Field(ge=1, le=5)`)
113	  becomes `{"causal_validity": payload.get("causal_validity"), ...}`. Right shape:
114	  `result.causal_validity` (attribute access on the model) or `result.model_dump()` straight into the
115	  TypedDict. The dict round-trip is a typing graveyard — schema drift on the Pydantic field name
116	  silently turns the value into `None` instead of failing. Fix at the boundary, not by adding more
117	  `.get` defaults.
118	  - **`graph: Any` when `BayesianGraph` exists** — `core/research_driver/data_model.py` defines a real
119	  `BayesianGraph` class with typed `nodes: dict[NodeId, Node]`, but the codebase has 119 occurrences of
120	  `graph: Any` in function signatures versus only 3 of `graph: BayesianGraph`. The `Any` papers over the
121	  `getattr(node, "type", None)` / `getattr(node, "data", {}) or {}` defensive access that follows. The
122	  reason it persists: tests mock graphs with `SimpleNamespace` because `BayesianGraph`'s constructor is
123	  heavy (requires `question_id`, `graphs_dir`, `graph_store_repository`, ...). Right fix: define a
124	  `Protocol` capturing the few node fields actually used and type the function against the protocol.
125	  Production passes real `BayesianGraph` (matches structurally), tests still pass `SimpleNamespace`
126	  (also matches), the `getattr` and `Any` go away. Tracked on SPI-256 as a codebase-wide refactor.
127	  - **`thing: object` parameter to dodge an import** — sharper variant of `graph: Any`. A function is
128	  designed for *one* concrete type (`tool: QuestionDrafterTool`, `result: FS10ValidationRunResult`,
129	  etc.) but typed as the bare `object` (or `Any`) to avoid importing the real class. Then the body
130	  reaches into the typed surface via `getattr(thing, "field", None)` and `if callable(...):` guards.
131	  Tell: the function only ever has one call site, and that call site passes an instance of the
132	  specific type. Worse when it accesses a *private* method (`getattr(tool, "_build_full_message_list",
133	  None)`) — that's reaching past the encapsulation boundary on top of throwing the type away. Right
134	  fix: import the type and type the parameter directly; if the import is genuinely circular, use a
135	  `Protocol` capturing the fields used. Promote private methods to public if external callers need
136	  them.
137	  - **Stringly-typed method lookup via `getattr(obj, "method_name", None)`** — looking up a method on
138	  a typed object by string name and storing the result as a `Callable | None`. Example:
139	  `load_nodes_for_question_run = getattr(self.graph_store_repository, "load_nodes_for_question_run",
140	  None)` followed by `if load_nodes_for_question_run is not None: load_nodes_for_question_run(...)`.
141	  Every smell stacks: the method name is a string literal (renames don't propagate, IDE find-usages
142	  misses it, mypy can't check it); the `None` fallback hides protocol drift (delete the method on
143	  the class, this code silently no-ops); the downstream `is not None` guards are defensive scaffolding
144	  for what should be a type contract. Three right shapes depending on intent: (a) if the method
145	  always exists on the type — delete the getattr, call it directly; (b) if it's genuinely optional
146	  across implementations — define a `Protocol` (with `@runtime_checkable` if test stubs need to opt
147	  out) and `isinstance`-check the object once at the boundary, the way `_PromptTraceableTool` was
148	  fixed in PR #221; (c) if it's a feature flag — make it an explicit constructor arg, not runtime
149	  introspection. Tell: the method name string is duplicated in the codebase (definition site + this
150	  lookup), and there's no Protocol declaring the optionality.
151	  - **Defensive `getattr` + `.get(...) or default` chains on typed dataclass / TypedDict returns** —
152	  even when the upstream return type is a typed dataclass or `TypedDict` (e.g.
153	  `FS10ValidationRunResult.run_score: RunScore | None`), it's common to see consumers write
154	  `getattr(run_score, "question_scores", [])`, `dict(getattr(question_score, "forecast_probabilities",
155	  {}) or {})`, `result.get("stage_b") or {}` everywhere. The defaults are dead code by construction
156	  if the return contract holds — and when the contract breaks, the silent default hides the bug.
157	  Right shape: handle the structural case once at the boundary (e.g. early-return if
158	  `result.run_score is None`), then use attribute access directly downstream. Pair-finding with the
159	  `graph: Any` entry above: a defensive-getattr chain almost always sits inside a function whose
160	  parameter is `Any` or `object`.
161	  - **Redundant filename prefixes that repeat the directory** — when a directory needs several related
162	  files, do not prefix every file with the same noun. The directory already supplies that context.
163	  Make it a subpackage and use short, scoped filenames. Reject filenames like
164	  `fs10_validation/real_tune_evaluator.py`, `fs10_validation/real_tune_run_lookup.py`,
165	  `fs10_validation/real_tune_metrics_fs2.py`, `fs10_validation/real_tune_metrics.py` — that
166	  `real_tune_` prefix is screaming for a subpackage. Use
167	  `fs10_validation/real_tune/{evaluator,run_lookup,metrics_fs2,metrics}.py` with an `__init__.py`
168	  that re-exports the public surface. Same rule applies anywhere else: if you find yourself
169	  prefixing N sibling files with the same word, lift the word into a directory.
170	  - **JSON sidecar files used as in-process IPC** — producer writes `audit_summary.json` (or similar)
171	  to disk and a consumer *in the same process* reads it back, often with `isinstance(x.get(k), dict)
172	  else {}` guards on the way in. Distinct from the "JSON files as storage" rule above, which is about
173	  persistence across runs: this is the *same-process* round-trip variant, where the disk hop has no
174	  consumer outside the process and exists only because two functions never agreed on an in-memory
175	  type. Right shape: pass the typed dataclass / TypedDict directly between the producer and consumer;
176	  if a durable on-disk artifact is also wanted, write it as a side effect from one well-defined
177	  serialisation point, not as the wire format between two collaborating functions.
178	  - **Functions with 7+ kwargs that should be one cohesive typed record** — distinct from the
179	  coercion-soup pattern above; the smell is the *signature shape itself*. Tells: the caller assembles
180	  a bag of pieces and the callee immediately disassembles it; typed objects and raw scalars are mixed
181	  in one signature (`built_count=len(stage_b_result.get(...))` alongside `stage_b_result`); kwargs
182	  with names like `*_lookup` / `*_result` / `*_payload` carry untyped dicts. `run_fs10_validation`
183	  at 16+ kwargs is the worst current offender. Right shape: define a `@dataclass` (or frozen
184	  `BaseModel`) for the call's inputs, build it once at the caller, pass it as a single parameter.
185	  Derived fields move onto the dataclass as `@property` or are computed inside the callee, not
186	  threaded as their own kwargs.
187	  - **Elide-on-falsy payload filters** — patterns like
188	  `{k: v for k, v in payload.items() if v not in (None, "", [], {})}` in hand-written `to_dict`
189	  / `as_*_payload` methods. Encodes "this field is optional in JSON" via a value-identity match
190	  instead of an explicit schema, conflating legitimate `0`, `0.0`, `False`, `segment_alpha=0` with
191	  unset. Right shape: declare optionality on a TypedDict / dataclass and serialise with a single
192	  rule (e.g. drop only `None`, never falsy zero / empty-string / empty-list, and only via a shared
193	  helper that the boundary owns).
194	  - **Identity self-mappings + unannotated alias renames inside one canonicalisation dict** —
195	  `qtype_map = {"binary": "binary", "categorical": "categorical", "numeric_ordinal":
196	  "numeric_bucket", ...}` overloads two jobs in one dict: identity canonicalisation *and* legacy
197	  alias resolution. No comment about why the legacy names exist or when they retire, and the lookup
198	  silently fails open via `.get(raw, "binary")`. Right shape: split into (a) a canonical enum, (b) a
199	  separate `LEGACY_ALIASES: dict[str, CanonicalType]` with a comment dating each alias and its
200	  removal plan, and (c) a lookup that raises on unknown values so regressions surface immediately.
201	  - **Stacked silent fail-open defaults (three layers deep)** — distinct from any single fallback in
202	  isolation; the smell is the *compounding* shape: `if not correct_answer: ... elif options ...
203	  else "Yes"`. When `correct_answer` is missing AND `resolved_outcome` isn't a bool AND `options` is
204	  empty, you silently get a fabricated `"Yes"`. Three layers of "make it work somehow" produce a
205	  plausible-looking value that no upstream contract justified. Right shape: collapse to one
206	  explicit boundary check at function entry, raise (or return a typed `Result.error`) when the
207	  required inputs aren't present, and let the caller decide the fallback.
208	  - **Magic date / numeric sentinels as parser defaults** — `_parse_package_date(..., default=
209	  date(2025, 1, 1))` / `date(2025, 12, 31)`. Either the default is dead code (the caller always
210	  provides a value) or it encodes an undocumented semantic ("missing start date means the start of
211	  time"). Both versions are bad: the dead-code version pollutes the API surface; the semantic
212	  version hides domain meaning in a literal. Right shape: drop the default and require the
213	  argument, or — if a sentinel really is needed — name it (`UNBOUNDED_START = date(2025, 1, 1)`)
214	  with a comment explaining what the parser does when the field is absent and why this particular
215	  date is safe.
216	  - **Undocumented hash truncation for synthetic row IDs** — `sha256(...).hexdigest()[:8]` or
217	  similar `[:N]` slices used as a row identifier. 32 bits collides at ~65k rows by the birthday
218	  bound; a silent collision means the second row overwrites pipeline state for the first. Right
219	  shape: either use the full digest (or a UUID), or — if a short ID is required for a real reason
220	  (logs, URLs) — pick a length large enough for the population size, name the constant, and add a
221	  comment justifying the collision budget. Pair with a uniqueness constraint at the DB level so a
222	  regression fails loudly instead of silently overwriting.
223	  - **Magic precision constants applied on both producer and consumer sides** — `round(x, 6)` at
224	  the producer *and* `round(x, 6)` at the consumer, with no shared constant. If anyone changes one
225	  side, the other drifts silently. Variant of the "magic numerical literals" rule, but the failure
226	  mode is cross-side: there is no single source of truth for the precision. Right shape: hoist to
227	  a module constant (`_FS2_PRICE_PRECISION = 6`) imported by both sides, or — better — round once
228	  at the persistence boundary and trust the value downstream.
229	  - **`int(...)` / `bool(...)` casts laundering already-typed `Mapped[...]` ORM columns** — sibling
230	  of the `str(x or "").strip()` rule but specifically about laundering already-typed values from
231	  typed ORM columns. Tell: inconsistency. If you see `int(row.foo)` wrapping 11 fields and notice
232	  the 12th (a `int | None` column) is *not* wrapped, the wraps are folklore — somebody copied the
233	  cast and propagated it without checking. Right shape: trust the `Mapped[int]` / `Mapped[bool]`
234	  type and write `row.foo` directly. Coerce only at boundaries where the source type genuinely
235	  differs (e.g. parsing JSON columns or external API responses).
236	  - **Multi-way fallback chains without a named authoritative field** —
237	  `row_key = str(row.question_id or row.candidate_id or row.external_id or title)`. The chain
238	  encodes a domain invariant ("one of these is always present, and they're ranked in this order")
239	  but no comment, type, or validator captures *which* field is authoritative, when each branch
240	  fires, or which combinations are invalid. Right shape: name the invariant. Either model the
241	  source as a tagged union (`row.identity: QuestionRef | CandidateRef | ExternalRef`) so the
242	  caller dispatches on type, or factor the chain into a `def _row_key(row) -> str` with a
243	  docstring explaining the precedence and an assertion / raise when none of the fields are set.
244	  - **Confusable sibling modules at the same package level** — e.g. a `components/` package
245	  sitting next to a `component_store.py` sibling at the same level. A reader cannot tell from the
246	  imports whether `component_store` is an alternative to `components/`, a layer above or below it,
247	  or unrelated. Variant of the redundant-filename-prefix rule applied to package-vs-sibling-file
248	  layouts. Right shape: fold the sibling into the package as `components/store.py` (or rename one
249	  side so the relationship is obvious), and re-export the public surface from `components/__init__.py`.
250	  - **DB-loaded vs file-loaded "same" field with different case / whitespace conventions** — every
251	  `.strip().upper()` / `.strip().lower()` against a stored string is a hint that some upstream
252	  persistence path didn't canonicalise the value. If `edge.type` is `"DIRECT"` when loaded from
253	  the DB but `"direct"` when loaded from a YAML fixture, the right fix is to normalise *at the
254	  write boundary* — once — so every downstream consumer sees the same shape. Right shape: enforce
255	  canonicalisation in the repository or serializer, not in every comparison site. Generalisation
256	  of the existing "defensive string wrapping on comparisons" rule with the extra step of asking
257	  *why* the values disagree in the first place.
258	  - **`BaseLLMTool.postprocess` reaching into its own typed `response_model` via stringly keys** —
259	  sharper, named-site variant of the typed-Pydantic→`dict.get` bridge already covered above.
260	  `postprocess` receives a model instance whose schema the tool *itself* declared via
261	  `response_model`, then accesses it as `payload.get("causal_validity")` instead of
262	  `response.causal_validity`. Known sweep target: grep `BaseLLMTool` subclasses' `postprocess`
263	  bodies for `payload.get(...)` and convert to attribute access on the declared response model.
264	  - **Two enum / Literal members that collapse to the same normalised record** —
265	  `QuestionType.BINARY` and `QuestionType.CATEGORICAL` produce identical post-normalisation
266	  records, which breeds defensive `if classification in {"binary", "categorical_outcome"}: ...`
267	  sets downstream. Distinct from generic stringly-typed code: the closed set itself has
268	  redundant members. Right shape: pick one canonical member, migrate the other (DB column +
269	  persisted JSON + callers) in a single change, and delete the set-membership checks that the
270	  duplication forced into existence.
271	  - **`print()` in domain code** — production paths (`core/`, `packages/`, `services/`,
272	  `fs10_validation/`) never call `print()`. Acceptable only in operator-facing CLI scripts under
273	  `tools/`. Tell: a `print(...)` call landing in a module imported by the pipeline. Right shape:
274	  module-level `logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)` and emit through that. If the intent was
275	  "see this in tests", assert on a return value or use pytest's `caplog`, not stdout.
276	  - **`datetime.utcnow()` or `datetime.now()` without `tz=`** — deprecated in Python 3.12+,
277	  produces a naive datetime, and lies about timezone semantics. Tell: any `datetime.utcnow()`
278	  or argumentless `datetime.now()` in a new diff. Right shape: `datetime.now(UTC)` (importing
279	  `UTC` from `datetime`), or call the shared `utc_now()` helper from
280	  `packages/storage/database.py:70-71` which guarantees a UTC-aware timestamp.
281	  - **`from typing import Optional / List / Dict / Tuple`** — the codebase targets Python 3.13
282	  and uses PEP 604 / builtin generics throughout. Tell: a `from typing import Optional` (or
283	  `List` / `Dict` / `Tuple`) line at the top of any non-legacy file. Right shape: builtin
284	  generics (`list[X]`, `dict[K, V]`) plus `X | None`.
285	  - **Modules without `from __future__ import annotations`** — every production module starts
286	  with this import (523 of ~570 modules already have it) to enable deferred annotation
287	  evaluation and forward references without quoting. Tell: a new file landing without the future
288	  import. Right shape: add it as the first import in every new module.
289	  - **Mutable defaults in dataclass fields or function arguments** — `field: list[X] = []`
290	  shares the list across all instances; `def f(x: list[int] = [])` shares it across all calls.
291	  Both are Python's mutable-default footgun. Tell: a dataclass field defaulted to `[]`, `{}`,
292	  `set()`, or a function arg with the same shape. Right shape: `field: list[X] =
293	  field(default_factory=list)`; for function args, default to `None` and assign inside the body.
294	  - **Mutable dataclasses without explicit justification** — the codebase defaults to
295	  `@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)` for value objects (~136 such dataclasses). Mutable
296	  dataclasses exist only where `__post_init__` has side effects or where ORM/inheritance forces
297	  it. Tell: a plain `@dataclass` decorator with no flags in a new file. Right shape:
298	  `@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)` by default; if mutability is required, add a one-line
299	  comment explaining why.
300	  - **Hand-rolled `Session(...)` / manual `commit()` / `rollback()`** — DB access goes through
301	  `database.session_scope()` as a context manager throughout `packages/storage/sql_repositories.py`.
302	  Tell: `with Session(self._engine) as session:` or manual commit/rollback in domain code.
303	  Right shape: `with self._database.session_scope() as session: ...` — commit/rollback are
304	  handled for you, with consistent error semantics.
305	  - **`@pytest.skip` decorators on individual tests for whole-module dependencies** — when an
306	  entire test file requires an optional module (e.g. `core.gjopen.pipeline`), skip the file at
307	  collection time via the `_OPTIONAL_TEST_DEPS` map in `tests/conftest.py`, not by decorating
308	  every function. Tell: many `@pytest.skip("module X missing")` decorators in one file. Right
309	  shape: add an entry to `_OPTIONAL_TEST_DEPS` and let conftest skip the whole module before
310	  collection.
311	  - **Mocking the database or storage interfaces in tests** — tests hit a real SQLite-in-memory
312	  database via fixtures like `graph_store_repo` in `tests/conftest.py:13-21`. Mocked DB tests
313	  silently diverge from production behaviour, especially across migrations. Tell:
314	  `unittest.mock.patch` against a repository method or a `Mock(spec=Session)` in a test. Right
315	  shape: declare the storage fixture as a test parameter and let pytest wire the real in-memory
316	  DB. If the fixture you need doesn't exist yet, add it to `conftest.py` — do not mock around it.
317	  - **Test fixture data loaded via relative string paths** — fixture JSON / YAML lives under
318	  `tests/data/` (recorded fixtures) or `tests/golden/` (snapshot baselines). Load via
319	  `Path(__file__).parent / "data" / "foo.json"`, not `"tests/data/foo.json"` (the latter breaks
320	  under any pytest CWD that isn't repo root). Tell: a string path passed to `open(...)` or
321	  `json.load(open(...))` inside a test. Right shape: anchor every fixture path to
322	  `Path(__file__).parent`.
323	  - **Raising bare `ValueError` / `Exception` from services or handlers** — the codebase has a
324	  typed exception hierarchy in `core/errors.py:16-23`: `DomainError(code, message, status_code)`
325	  with subclasses `NotFoundError(404)`, `ConflictError(409)`, `ValidationError(422)`,
326	  `PipelineError(500)`. Tell: `raise ValueError("question not found")` in a service. Right
327	  shape: `raise NotFoundError("question.not_found", f"question {qid} not found")` — the code
328	  string is greppable, the status maps cleanly to HTTP responses, and structured context flows
329	  through the logger.
330	  - **Re-raising exceptions without `from`** — when catching and re-raising, the original
331	  exception must be preserved as `__cause__` so the traceback shows the root cause. Tell:
332	  `raise NewError(...)` inside an `except OldError:` block with no `from`. Right shape: `raise
333	  NewError(...) from original`. (Use `from None` only to deliberately hide the original, and
334	  add a comment explaining why.)
335	  - **Logger created inside a function body** — `def f(): logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)`
336	  is wasted work; the logger is a module-global singleton anyway. Tell:
337	  `logging.getLogger(__name__)` inside any function body. Right shape: one module-level
338	  `logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)` at the top of every file that needs to log.
339	  - **Datetime JSON serialisation as `+00:00`** — the codebase normalises UTC offsets to the
340	  `Z` suffix in serialised payloads and log records. Tell: an `.isoformat()` call landing
341	  `+00:00` into a stored JSON column, API response, or log line. Right shape:
342	  `.isoformat().replace("+00:00", "Z")`, or use a shared helper that does the normalisation
343	  once at the boundary.
344	  - **`os.path.join` / string concatenation for filesystem paths** — `pathlib.Path` is used
345	  throughout production code (~208 `pathlib` imports vs ~88 `os.path.*`, mostly in legacy CLI
346	  helpers). Tell: `os.path.join(base, "subdir", "file.json")` in a new diff. Right shape:
347	  `Path(base) / "subdir" / "file.json"`. `os.path.*` is acceptable inside `tools/` scripts but
348	  not in `core/`, `packages/`, `services/`, or `fs10_validation/`.
349	  - **Relative imports in production code** — production code uses absolute imports throughout
350	  (`from packages.storage.database import ...`). Relative imports (`from .foo import ...`)
351	  appear ~28 times and are confined to older code. Tell: a `from .` line in a new file outside
352	  legacy areas. Right shape: absolute import from the package root.
353	  - **`from X import *`** — zero star imports in production code; the rule is uncontroversial
354	  but worth restating. Tell: a star import in a new diff. Right shape: explicit import list.
355	  - **`async def` in non-I/O code paths** — the codebase is sync-by-default. Async is reserved
356	  for FastAPI route handlers and a few I/O boundaries (~17 `async def` total, mostly in
357	  `services/`). Tell: `async def` on a pure-compute helper or domain service. Right shape: keep
358	  it sync; if a downstream call is async, isolate the async boundary at the route layer and
359	  pass plain data inward.
360	  - **`BaseModel` accepting unknown fields** — API DTOs in `packages/contracts/` set
361	  `model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid")` so schema drift fails loudly instead of silently
362	  dropping fields (see `packages/contracts/jobs.py:37-44`). Tell: a new `BaseModel` without
363	  `extra="forbid"`. Right shape: forbid extras by default; if a permissive model is genuinely
364	  required (transitional payload, third-party webhook), document that in the docstring.
365	  - **`BaseModel` for internal domain / storage types** — `BaseModel` belongs at API boundaries
366	  (`packages/contracts/`, LLM tool input/output, request/response). Domain entities, storage
367	  records (`Stored*` types), and value objects use `@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)`. Tell:
368	  a `class Foo(BaseModel)` for a record returned from a repository. Right shape:
369	  `@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)`.
370	  - **Package `__init__.py` without `__all__`** — every package `__init__.py` declares its
371	  public surface via `__all__` (see `packages/contracts/__init__.py:92`,
372	  `packages/auth/__init__.py:14-27`). Internal submodules can refactor freely as long as
373	  `__init__.py` keeps re-exporting the same names. Tell: a new package whose `__init__.py` has
374	  imports but no `__all__`. Right shape: list every public re-export in `__all__`.
375	  - **Eager imports in `__init__.py` for heavy packages** — for packages with many sub-modules
376	  (e.g. `packages/contracts/`), eagerly importing every sub-module at package load is wasteful
377	  and creates circular-import risk. Tell: 20+ `from .foo import Foo` lines at the top of
378	  `__init__.py`. Right shape: lazy `__getattr__(name)` plus a `_SYMBOL_TO_MODULE` dict that
379	  imports on first access and caches in `globals()` — see `packages/contracts/__init__.py:82-89`.
380	  - **`os.environ.get(...)` outside `core/settings.py`** — sharpened restatement of the
381	  existing "`os.environ` deep in domain code" rule. All env reads go through
382	  `core.settings.env_text(...)`, `env_int(...)`, `env_bool(...)`, `path_from_env(...)` (the
383	  module also auto-loads `.env` at import time). Domain code consumes typed config dataclasses
384	  (e.g. `StorageRuntimeConfig`) that were built once at startup. Tell: `os.environ.get("...")`
385	  in any file outside `core/settings.py` or a top-level `main.py` / CLI entry point. Right
386	  shape: thread the typed config in.
387	  - **Config wired through module-level globals** — `StorageRuntimeConfig` (and siblings) are
388	  built once at app startup and passed into constructors (see `tests/conftest.py:13-21` for the
389	  test-side pattern). Tell: a module-level `_STORAGE_CONFIG = load_storage_config()` import
390	  side-effect, or a `get_storage_config()` global singleton. Right shape: pass the config as a
391	  constructor argument and let DI carry it from the entry point downward.
392	  - **Logging via `print(f"...")` or unstructured string interpolation** — the codebase emits
393	  structured JSON logs through `core.logging.JSONFormatter` with contextvars
394	  (`set_run_context`, `set_trace_id`) injecting `trace_id` / `run_id` / `question_id` /
395	  `step_id` / `user_id` / `document_key` automatically. Tell: `logger.info(f"finished run
396	  {run_id}")` instead of `logger.info("run.finished", extra={"run_id": run_id})`. Right shape:
397	  short event names in the message field, structured payload in `extra=`. The formatter will
398	  emit a single line of JSON downstream tooling can index.
399	  - **Pydantic v1 idioms (`.dict()`, `.parse_obj()`, `Config` inner class)** — the project is
400	  on Pydantic v2. Tell: a `class Config: ...` block inside a `BaseModel`, or `.dict()` /
401	  `.json()` / `.parse_obj()` calls. Right shape: `model_config = ConfigDict(...)`,
402	  `model_dump()`, `model_dump_json()`, `model_validate(...)`.
403	  - **`TODO` / `FIXME` / `XXX` comments without a ticket reference or owner** — these are fine
404	  as in-flight markers if they name a Linear issue (`# TODO(SPI-256): ...`) but become noise
405	  otherwise. Tell: a bare `# TODO: clean up later`. Right shape: link the follow-up ticket and
406	  state the trigger condition, or open a Linear issue now and leave the link in the comment.
407	  Bias toward fixing the thing instead of leaving a marker.
408	
409	  ### Architectural patterns — always follow
410	
411	  **Storage / persistence**
412	
413	  - LLM tools inherit from `BaseLLMTool` and every `LLMConfig()` must have `task_id=`.
414	  - Use `resolve_task_model("FS3.task")` for named tasks, `resolve_role_model("tool"|"agent")`
415	  for generic fallbacks. Never hardcode model names.
416	  - All stage results use `build_stage_outcome()`. Never return ad-hoc dicts.
417	  - All graph nodes use `build_node_data()` / `build_origin()` / `build_provenance()`.
418	  - Direct SQL access is confined to `packages/storage/database.py`. Domain code never touches
419	  SQLAlchemy sessions or raw SQL directly — it goes through repositories that return stored
420	  dataclasses.
421	  - All DB access uses `database.session_scope()` as a context manager; commit / rollback are
422	  handled by the context manager, never by hand.
423	  - Repositories expose typed methods like
424	  `XxxRepository.list_for_question(qid) -> list[StoredXxx]`. Row classes never leave the
425	  storage package.
426	  - Row ↔ record conversions live as private module helpers named
427	  `_<entity>_row_to_record(row) -> StoredX` and `_<entity>_record_to_row(record) -> XRow`
428	  (see `packages/storage/sql_repositories.py:283-350`). Don't inline the conversion at the
429	  call site.
430	  - Writes into JSON columns go through type-specific sanitizers
431	  (`_payload_text`, `_payload_int`, `_payload_bool`, `_payload_list` in
432	  `packages/storage/sql_repositories.py:100-135`) — domain code never `.get()`-coerces values
433	  before persisting.
434	  - Alembic migrations are named `YYYYMMDD_NN_<short_description>.py` and are idempotent —
435	  use the `_has_table()` / `_index_names()` inspector helpers (see
436	  `alembic/versions/20260518_01_research_driver_stage_artifacts.py`) so reruns are safe.
437	
438	  **Type system**
439	
440	  - Every production module starts with `from __future__ import annotations`.
441	  - Use PEP 604 `X | None` everywhere; never `Optional[X]`. Builtin generics (`list[X]`,
442	  `dict[K, V]`, `tuple[A, B]`) over `typing.List` / `typing.Dict` / `typing.Tuple`.
443	  - Default to `@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)` for value objects.
444	  - `BaseModel` is for API and LLM boundaries; `@dataclass` is for domain / storage / value
445	  objects.
446	  - Use `TYPE_CHECKING` blocks for circular-import dodges and Protocol-only imports
447	  (`packages/storage/interfaces.py`, `core/research/tool.py`).
448	  - Mutable dataclass / function-arg defaults use `field(default_factory=...)` or default to
449	  `None` and assign inside; never literal `[]` / `{}` / `set()`.
450	
451	  **Time, paths, IO**
452	
453	  - UTC timestamps everywhere: `datetime.now(UTC)` or the shared `utc_now()` helper from
454	  `packages/storage/database.py`. Never `datetime.utcnow()`, never naive datetimes at storage
455	  boundaries.
456	  - JSON datetime serialisation uses the `Z` suffix:
457	  `dt.isoformat().replace("+00:00", "Z")`. The reverse-parse must accept both `Z` and `+00:00`.
458	  - `pathlib.Path` everywhere in production code. `os.path.*` is reserved for `tools/`
459	  scripts.
460	
461	  **Logging and errors**
462	
463	  - One module-level `logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)` per file. Emit structured logs
464	  via `extra={...}`, not via f-string interpolation into the message field.
465	  - Use `core.logging.set_run_context()` / `set_trace_id()` to inject contextvars at request
466	  / job entry points; downstream logs pick up `trace_id` / `run_id` / `question_id`
467	  automatically.
468	  - Raise `DomainError` subclasses (`NotFoundError`, `ConflictError`, `ValidationError`,
469	  `PipelineError`) with explicit code + message + status_code (see `core/errors.py:16-23`).
470	  Never raise bare `ValueError` / `Exception` from a service or handler.
471	  - Preserve traceback chains with `raise NewError(...) from original`. `from None` only with
472	  a comment explaining why.
473	
474	  **Imports and packages**
475	
476	  - Absolute imports only. No star imports. Relative imports are confined to legacy code and
477	  are not added in new files.
478	  - Every package `__init__.py` declares its public surface in `__all__`. Internal submodule
479	  refactors are free as long as the re-exports stay stable.
480	  - For packages with many sub-modules, defer imports via `__getattr__(name)` plus a
481	  `_SYMBOL_TO_MODULE` dict (pattern at `packages/contracts/__init__.py:82-89`).
482	
483	  **Config**
484	
485	  - All `os.environ` reads happen inside `core/settings.py` via `env_text(...)`,
486	  `env_int(...)`, `env_bool(...)`, `path_from_env(...)`. `.env` is auto-loaded at import time.
487	  - Storage / app config is a typed dataclass (`StorageRuntimeConfig` and siblings) built
488	  once at startup and passed to constructors. No global singletons or module-level
489	  side-effect loads in domain code.
490	
491	  **Testing**
492	
493	  - Tests hit a real in-memory SQLite database via fixtures like `graph_store_repo` in
494	  `tests/conftest.py`. Do not mock `Session` / repositories.
495	  - Skip entire test modules at collection time via the `_OPTIONAL_TEST_DEPS` map in
496	  `tests/conftest.py`; do not scatter `@pytest.skip` over individual functions.
497	  - Group related tests as `class Test<Feature>:` with `test_<subject>_<expectation>`
498	  methods.
499	  - Fixture data lives under `tests/data/` (recordings) or `tests/golden/` (snapshots),
500	  loaded via `Path(__file__).parent / "data" / "..."` — never relative string paths.
501	  - Common test setup (storage config, repo wiring) goes in `tests/conftest.py` as
502	  `@pytest.fixture`, not in per-file `_helper(tmp_path)` shims.
503	
504	  **Async**
505	
506	  - Sync by default. Async is allowed only at FastAPI route handlers and other I/O boundaries
507	  (~17 `async def` total in the codebase). Domain services and pure-compute helpers are sync.
508	
509	  **Tooling**
510	
511	  - Ruff lint rules enabled: `E`, `F`, `I`, `UP` (see `pyproject.toml:75-76`). `E501`
512	  line-length is ignored project-wide; the soft 140-char limit is enforced by review.
513	  - MyPy is soft-mode globally with per-module strict overrides for storage-critical packages
514	  (see `pyproject.toml:81-99`). Domain code is type-hinted by convention even where MyPy is
515	  permissive.
516	
```

> AGENT

Yes — I can see `codequality.md` (the "Anti-patterns — reject on sight" catalogue) and `agent_rules/pr_workflow.md`. Reading the diff against that list, I missed several violations on my first pass. Re-reviewing PR #432 strictly against the named anti-patterns:

## Anti-patterns in PR #432 (cited)

**1. `graph: Any` / `node: Any` everywhere — `graph: Any` rule** (`graph_marginalisation_validation.py`)

The entire new module types its inputs as `Any`:
```python
def validate_graph_for_marginalisation(*, question_id: str, nodes: list[Any], edges: list[Any]):
def _node_id(node: Any) -> str:
def _node_label(node: Any) -> str:
def _node_type(node: Any) -> str:
def _node_data(node: Any) -> dict[str, Any]:
def _edge_id(edge: Any) -> str:
def _edge_type(edge: Any) -> str:
def _edge_from(edge: Any) -> str:
def _edge_to(edge: Any) -> str:
def _edge_weight(edge: Any) -> float | None:
```
Followed by exactly the `getattr(node, "type", None)` / `getattr(node, "data", {}) or {}` chain the doc warns about. `NodeRecord` and `EdgeRecord` are real typed records (the test file imports them). Either type against those directly or define a Protocol — don't `Any` your way past the import.

**2. Defensive string wrapping on comparisons — `str(x or "").strip().upper()` rule** (`graph_marginalisation_validation.py`)

Literal textbook example:
```python
def _edge_type(edge: Any) -> str:
    raw = getattr(edge, "type", None)
    if raw is None:
        raw = getattr(edge, "toi", None)
    return str(raw or "").strip().upper()
# ...
if _edge_type(edge) != "CONDITIONAL":
```
Same pattern in `_node_type`, `_node_id`, `_node_label`, `_edge_id`, `_edge_from`, `_edge_to`. If `EdgeRecord.type` is canonicalised at the write boundary (and it should be), `edge.type == "CONDITIONAL"` is the right shape. If it isn't, fix the persistence side — don't `.strip().upper()` at every read site.

**3. Defensive `getattr` + `.get(...) or default` on typed records** (`graph_marginalisation_validation.py`)
```python
def _node_baseline_p(node: Any) -> float | None:
    raw = getattr(node, "baseline_p", None)
    if raw is None:
        return None
    try:
        return float(raw)
    except (TypeError, ValueError):
        return None
```
`NodeRecord.baseline_p` is `float | None`. The `try/except` and the `float(raw)` cast are dead code — same anti-pattern as `int(row.foo)` laundering already-typed columns. `_edge_weight` and `manual_posterior_override_p` have the same shape.

**4. Magic precision constants applied on both producer and consumer sides — direct rule**
```python
# node_data.py:57
"posterior_p": round(clamped, 6),
# bn_inference.py:_write_posteriors_to_graph
setattr(node, "posterior_p", round(float(override_p), 6))
```
Two `round(x, 6)` sites, no shared constant. The doc names this exact pattern: hoist to a module constant or round once at the boundary.

**5. Private cross-module imports** (`graph_marginalisation_validation.py:4`)
```python
from core.research_driver.bn_inference import _ancestor_closure, _build_parent_maps, _topological_order_or_raise
```
Not a named codequality rule but obviously wrong — reaching past three underscore boundaries to build a public surface in a new module.

**6. Two-location lookup for the same field** (`graph_marginalisation_validation.py:550-552`)
```python
not isinstance(_node_data(node).get("detail", {}).get("option_index"), int)
and not isinstance(_node_data(node).get("option_index"), int)
```
This is the "DB-loaded vs file-loaded 'same' field with different conventions" rule applied to dict shape. Pick one storage location and enforce at the write boundary.

**7. From my earlier review (still valid):**
- `build_manual_posterior_override` clamp is silently undone by `round(., 6)`.
- `without_manual_posterior_override` doesn't remove the key, it disables it (misleading name).
- PR title `UI:` is not a permitted prefix (`pr_workflow.md:50-54`: `FS<N>:` / `Cross-FS:` / `#<issue>:`).

The new validation module is essentially a textbook of the anti-patterns the doc lists. I'd push back on this and ask for it to be re-typed against `NodeRecord` / `EdgeRecord` (or a `Protocol`) before merge — the cleanup is mechanical but should happen now, not as a follow-up.

> AGENT

Scope check — not a lot of work. Most of it is mechanical:

**Findings that affect plan:**
- Validation module has zero production callers (only its own tests) → free to change the interface.
- Two real shapes need to be handled: `NodeRecord`/`EdgeRecord` (storage) and `Node`/`Edge` (in-memory `BayesianGraph`) — they use different field names (`node_id` vs `id`, `from_node` vs `from_id`). So we need either two Protocols or normalization at the `validate_graph_object_for_marginalisation` boundary.
- `option_index` canonical home is `data.detail.option_index` — single location, use the existing `node_detail_value` helper.

**Estimated work (~1–2h):**

1. Promote `_ancestor_closure`, `_build_parent_maps`, `_topological_order_or_raise` → public in `bn_inference.py` (~5 min)
2. Hoist `round(., 6)` precision to a module constant in `node_data.py`, share both producer and consumer sites (~5 min)
3. Fix the clamp-undone-by-round bug; rename `without_*` → `disable_*` (~10 min)
4. Rewrite `graph_marginalisation_validation.py`: define a `MarginalisationNode` / `MarginalisationEdge` Protocol matching `NodeRecord`/`EdgeRecord`, drop all `Any` + `getattr` + `str().strip().upper()` defensive scaffolding, drop dead `try/except` on typed floats, use single canonical `option_index` location (~30–45 min — biggest piece)
5. Adapt `validate_graph_object_for_marginalisation` to convert `Node`/`Edge` → record shape at the boundary (~10 min)
6. Update both test files; ensure new tests still cover everything (~15 min)
7. Run lint + tests; push to the PR branch (~10 min)

One question before I start:

```
174	def _topological_order_or_raise(*, active_nodes: set[str], edges: list[tuple[str, str, float]]) -> list[str]:
175	    from collections import deque
176	
177	    parents_by_child, children_by_parent = _build_parent_maps(edges, active_nodes=active_nodes)
178	    indegree: dict[str, int] = {node_id: len(parents_by_child.get(node_id, [])) for node_id in active_nodes}
179	    queue: deque[str] = deque(sorted(node_id for node_id, degree in indegree.items() if degree == 0))
180	    order: list[str] = []
181	    visited: set[str] = set()
182	    while queue:
183	        node_id = queue.popleft()
184	        if node_id in visited:
185	            continue
186	        visited.add(node_id)
187	        order.append(node_id)
188	        for child in children_by_parent.get(node_id, []):
189	            indegree[child] = max(0, indegree[child] - 1)
190	            if indegree[child] == 0 and child not in visited:
191	                queue.append(child)
192	    if len(order) != len(active_nodes):
193	        remaining = sorted(node_id for node_id in active_nodes if node_id not in visited)
194	        raise ValueError(f"CONDITIONAL subgraph must be acyclic; cycle detected among nodes: {remaining[:12]}")
195	    return order
196

204	    stack = list(targets)
205	    while stack:
206	        node_id = stack.pop()
207	        for parent_id in parents_by_child.get(node_id, []):
```

```
287	    if not manual_posterior_override_enabled(data):
288	        return None
289	    payload = manual_posterior_override_payload(data)
290	    try:
291	        return float(payload.get("posterior_p"))
292	    except (TypeError, ValueError):
293	        return None
294	
295	
296	def build_manual_posterior_override(
297	    *,
298	    posterior_p: float,
299	    source: str = "user",
300	    updated_at: str,
301	    enabled: bool = True,
302	) -> dict[str, Any]:
303	    clamped = min(1.0 - 1e-9, max(1e-9, float(posterior_p)))
304	    return {
305	        "enabled": bool(enabled),
306	        "posterior_p": round(clamped, 6),
307	        "source": str(source or "user").strip() or "user",
308	        "updated_at": str(updated_at or "").strip(),
309	    }
310	
311	
312	def with_manual_posterior_override(
313	    data: Mapping[str, Any] | None,
314	    *,
315	    posterior_p: float,
316	    source: str = "user",
317	    updated_at: str,
318	) -> dict[str, Any]:
319	    payload = dict(data or {})
320	    payload[MANUAL_POSTERIOR_OVERRIDE_KEY] = build_manual_posterior_override(
321	        posterior_p=posterior_p,
322	        source=source,
323	        updated_at=updated_at,
324	    )
325	    return payload
326	
327	
328	def without_manual_posterior_override(data: Mapping[str, Any] | None) -> dict[str, Any]:
329	    payload = dict(data or {})
330	    override = dict(payload.get(MANUAL_POSTERIOR_OVERRIDE_KEY) or {})
331	    if override:
332	        override["enabled"] = False
333	        payload[MANUAL_POSTERIOR_OVERRIDE_KEY] = override
334	    return payload
335	
336	
337	def resolve_manual_posterior_override_p(
338	    data: Mapping[str, Any] | None,
339	    *,
340	    posterior_p: float | None = None,
341	    fallback_posterior_p: float | None = None,
342	) -> float:
343	    if posterior_p is not None:
344	        return float(posterior_p)
345	    payload = manual_posterior_override_payload(data)
346	    if _is_present(payload.get("posterior_p")):
347	        return float(payload["posterior_p"])
348	    if fallback_posterior_p is not None:
349	        return float(fallback_posterior_p)
350	    raise ValueError("posterior_p required to enable manual posterior override")
351	
352	
353	def set_manual_posterior_override_enabled(
354	    data: Mapping[str, Any] | None,
355	    *,
356	    enabled: bool,
357	    updated_at: str,
358	    posterior_p: float | None = None,
359	    fallback_posterior_p: float | None = None,
360	) -> dict[str, Any]:
361	    payload = dict(data or {})
362	    if not enabled:
363	        return without_manual_posterior_override(payload)
364	    existing = manual_posterior_override_payload(payload)
365	    resolved = resolve_manual_posterior_override_p(
366	        payload,
367	        posterior_p=posterior_p,
368	        fallback_posterior_p=fallback_posterior_p,
369	    )
370	    payload[MANUAL_POSTERIOR_OVERRIDE_KEY] = build_manual_posterior_override(
371	        posterior_p=resolved,
372	        source=str(existing.get("source") or "user"),
373	        updated_at=updated_at,
374	    )
375	    return payload
376	
```

```
563	def _write_posteriors_to_graph(
564	    *,
565	    question_nodes: dict[str, Any],
566	    active_nodes: set[str],
567	    posterior_by_target: dict[str, float],
568	    node_posteriors: dict[str, float],
569	) -> None:
570	    """Write computed posteriors back to graph node attributes."""
571	    for node_id, node in question_nodes.items():
572	        if node_id not in active_nodes:
573	            continue
574	        node_data = getattr(node, "data", {}) or {}
575	        if manual_posterior_override_enabled(node_data):
576	            override_p = manual_posterior_override_p(node_data)
577	            if override_p is not None:
578	                setattr(node, "posterior_p", round(float(override_p), 6))
579	            continue
580	        # IW trigger clamp — do not overwrite
581	        _iw = node_data.get("iw_trigger") or {}
582	        if _iw.get("verdict") in ("TRUE", "FALSE"):
583	            continue
584	        if node_id in posterior_by_target:
585	            setattr(node, "posterior_p", round(float(posterior_by_target[node_id]), 6))
586	        else:
587	            setattr(node, "posterior_p", round(float(node_posteriors.get(node_id, 0.0)), 6))
588
```
